LETTER TO THE SHAREHOLDERS
Dear Shareholders, 
Ladies and Gentlemen,
Klöckner & Co once again had to hold its own in a difficult 
economic environment in the past fiscal year. Above all, 
geopolitical tensions and disruptions in global supply 
chains resulted in uncertainties and challenges in the core 
markets relevant to Klöckner & Co, especially in Europe. 
Demand for steel declined significantly in Europe. The 
reporting period also saw a sharp fall in steel prices both 
in the USA and in Europe. This environment had a 
corresponding impact on our business activities. At the 
same time, Klöckner & Co continued to develop as a 
company and made significant progress in implementing 
the Group strategy.
Our acquisitions in the USA and Mexico in particular 
meant that we were able to increase shipments to 
4.5 million tons. Due to the unfavorable price trend, 
however, sales declined slightly to €6.6 billion in the full 
year 2024. In the past fiscal year, operating income 
(EBITDA) before material special effects amounted to 
€136 million, while continuing operations recorded a net 
loss of €-146 million. This resulted in negative earnings 
per share attributable to shareholders of Klöckner & Co SE 
of €-1.47. Cash flow from operating activities, on the 
other hand, was clearly positive at €160 million. Given 
that our operating cash flow was positive for the third 
year in a row and that only part of the net income 
generated in previous years was distributed, we will be 
proposing a dividend of €0.20 per share at the Annual 
General Meeting.
Continued consistent implementation of corporate 
strategy
The basis of our success is the systematic implementation 
of our Group strategy. We aim to position Klöckner & Co 
as the leading one-stop shop for steel and metal products 
as well as higher value-added processing in North America 
and Europe. Furthermore, we want to establish 
sustainable business models and further reduce our 
environmental impact and carbon footprint. Through 
targeted investment at selected sites in the USA and 
Germany, we have been able to expand the higher value-
added processing and metalworking business in the 
reporting year. Looking ahead, this is an important step 
towards reducing our earnings volatility and increasing 
profitability. We also made good progress in the key 
strategic areas of digitalization and automation, and 
continue to work systematically at generating value with 
minimum manual effort.
“The basis of our success is the systematic 
implementation of our Group strategy. 
We aim to position Klöckner & Co as the 
leading one-stop shop for steel and metal 
products as well as higher value-added 
processing in North America and Europe."
Guido Kerkhoff
CHIEF EXECUTIVE OFFICER (CEO)
Increased focus on higher value-added business
In the past 2024 fiscal year, we took numerous measures 
enabling us to further enhance our focus on higher value-
added business. This is characterized by long-term 
contracts and a high degree of customer specialization, 
while offering higher profitability and more stable 
demand. Going forward, this will significantly reduce our 
exposure to volatile steel price trends.
4
KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

In this context, the successful completion of the sale of 
the country organizations in France, the United Kingdom, 
the Netherlands and Belgium in the first half of 2024 was 
an important milestone. With this strategic decision, we 
have increased our focus on higher value-added business 
and reduced our exposure to volatile, low-margin 
distribution. As a result, the regional focus has also shifted 
to North America, which is by far the biggest market, and 
the attractive “DACH” region (Germany, Austria and 
Switzerland) in Europe. This realignment not only 
improves our business performance, but also opens up 
new opportunities for further growth.
Long-term success through strategic investments and 
organic growth
After our acquisitions of National Material of Mexico 
(“NMM”) and Industrial Manufacturing Services (“IMS”) in 
2023, we increased our focus on organic growth in fiscal 
year 2024. This step is again in line with our goal of 
focusing more closely on the higher value-added 
processing and metalworking business in North America 
and Europe. We have thus systematically transformed our 
Charlotte and Dallas sites from distribution centers into 
production and processing centers for higher value-added 
metal solutions.
Just recently, approval was granted for our investment in a 
new aluminum processing plant on the site of the 
aluminum flat rolling mill currently under construction for 
Aluminum Dynamics LLC (ADL) in Columbus, Mississippi 
(USA). The plant is scheduled to go into operation in 2026 
and will be specialized in the production and processing of 
aluminum profiles. This enables us to focus specifically on 
sustainable lightweight solutions and aluminum products 
that are becoming increasingly important for the energy 
and automotive sectors. The strategic investment is being 
made by Kloeckner Metals Corporation (KMC), our US 
subsidiary, and will accelerate growth in the USA and 
Mexico. The investment also strengthens our long-
standing relationship with ADL’s parent company, Steel 
Dynamics Inc (SDI).
In Europe, too, we want to continue growing in higher 
value-added business, and we made significant progress in 
the past year towards achieving this goal. An example of 
this is the investment in a fully automated sawing and 
drilling system in Landsberg. In addition, the Kassel site 
has brought into operation one of Europe’s most 
advanced laser cutting systems, enabling highly precise 
and efficient metal processing. 
Position consolidated as pioneer of a sustainable steel 
industry
At Klöckner & Co, we see the decarbonization of the steel 
industry as a strategic opportunity for ourselves and our 
customers. As part of our sustainability strategy, under 
the Nexigen® umbrella brand, we provide customers with 
transparent, CO2-reduced solutions in the categories of 
materials, processing, logistics and service solutions that 
aid in the establishment of sustainable value chains. The 
successful delivery of first batches of CO2-reduced 
stainless steel to technology group GEA in 2024 
underscores the successful implementation of this 
ambition.
With the Nexigen® PCF Algorithm, we have further 
advanced an innovative tool whose PCF calculation 
methodology is certified by TÜV SÜD. The web-based 
technology 
solution 
based 
on 
this 
algorithm, 
Nexigen® Data Services, makes the carbon emission data 
of the steel and metal products purchased from 
Klöckner & Co transparent to customers and offers CO2-
reduced product alternatives. Customers are actively 
supported in their decarbonization efforts, allowing them 
to make more environmentally conscious decisions.
Our sustainability activities have also once again been 
recognized by external sources in 2024. Klöckner & Co 
won the German Sustainability Award for the second 
time – Europe’s most prestigious award for environmental 
and social commitment. The repeat award highlights the 
fact that we have successfully integrated sustainability 
into our business model and are thus making an effective 
contribution to transformation in the long term.

Sustainability also plays an important role inside 
Klöckner & Co. Over the turn of the year 2024/2025, we 
moved into our new corporate headquarters in Airport 
Garden at Düsseldorf Airport. Airport Garden is a 
sustainability-certified building and offers a modern and 
future-oriented working environment.
Major progress in digitalization and automation
Digitalization and automation are important levers in our 
Group strategy. By automating sub-processes, we gain a 
competitive advantage and enhance the scalability of our 
business model. This facilitates our integration of acquired 
companies and thus also provides a good foundation for 
long-term profitability. A key to these efforts is Kloeckner 
Assistant, which automates sales processes and reduces 
the number of manual interventions per order. Our vision 
is “zero touch,” meaning value generation with minimum 
manual effort. During the reporting period, we further 
improved the efficiency of our digital ordering processes 
in the relevant organizational units and increased the 
number of digital quotes by more than 26% compared to 
the prior-year period.
We made numerous strategic decisions in fiscal year 2024 
to ensure our Company’s long-term future success. A key 
part in this success is played by the Klöckner & Co 
workforce. I would like to take this opportunity to thank 
them for their tireless efforts. With their daily work, they 
all ensure that we are and remain a strong and reliable 
partner to our customers.
I would also like to thank our Supervisory Board for their 
continuing support and advice in shaping our Company. 
And I thank you, our valued shareholders, for sharing this 
journey with us and placing your trust in Klöckner & Co.
Sincerely,
Guido Kerkhoff
CHIEF EXECUTIVE OFFICER (CEO)

REPORT OF THE SUPERVISORY BOARD
During the reporting year, the Supervisory Board once 
again performed, with due care, the duties incumbent 
upon it by law, the Company’s Articles of Association and 
the Rules of Procedure.
The Supervisory Board supervised and regularly advised 
the Management Board, and satisfied itself that the 
Management Board’s decisions and actions were legally 
compliant, orderly and fit for purpose. Where appropriate, 
the Supervisory Board consulted external experts and 
relevant 
studies. 
The 
Supervisory 
Board 
adopted 
resolutions as required by law, the Articles of Association 
or the Rules of Procedure, in each instance after thorough 
and careful appraisal. This notably included transactions 
and measures for which the Articles of Association and/or 
Rules of Procedure require the Management Board to 
obtain Supervisory Board approval; after in-depth 
consultation, the Supervisory Board granted the required 
approval in each case.
The Supervisory Board was involved on a timely basis in all 
matters of fundamental importance. To this end, the 
Management Board provided the Supervisory Board with 
information on planning, the Company’s business and 
financial situation, and all business transactions of 
importance to the Company and the Group, both in and 
between Supervisory Board meetings. Supervisory Board 
meetings regularly included reports on the overall 
economic climate, the industry situation and the business 
performance of the Klöckner & Co Group and its 
segments, 
key 
performance 
indicators 
and 
the 
performance of the Klöckner & Co share price. Risk 
exposure, risk management, the internal control system 
and 
compliance 
(including 
data 
protection 
and 
information security) and sustainability topics were also 
covered in detail. Corporate strategy, among other 
matters, was discussed in particular detail at the 
September meeting. In all instances, the Supervisory 
Board was supplied with comprehensive documentation.
Both in plenary sessions and in committee meetings, the 
members of the Supervisory Board thoroughly reviewed 
the Management Board’s reports and intended actions 
and made various suggestions. Information was also 
exchanged on a regular basis between meetings. Written 
reporting by the Management Board during the reporting 
year once again centered on the detailed monthly Board 
Reports. Independently of this, the CEO, in most cases 
together with the CFO, held personal meetings with the 
Chairman of the Supervisory Board on a monthly basis to 
report on current business developments, salient issues 
and upcoming decisions, to discuss them with him and 
decide on the further course of action.
Organization of the Supervisory Board’s work
The six-member Supervisory Board is made up entirely of 
shareholder representatives. The Supervisory Board has 
established two committees to carry out its duties: a 
Presidium and an Audit Committee, each with three 
members.
The members of the Supervisory Board are Prof. Dr. Dieter 
H. Vogel (Chairman), Dr. Ralph Heck (Deputy Chairman), 
Prof. Dr. Tobias Kollmann, Prof. Dr. E.h. Friedhelm Loh, 
Uwe Röhrhoff and Dagmar Steinert. All of the Supervisory 
Board members have many years of experience on 
management and/or supervisory bodies of various entities 
and, with their expertise, together very effectively cover 
the full range of responsibilities required by the Company. 
The Presidium comprises Prof. Dr. Dieter H. Vogel 
(Chairman), Dr. Ralph Heck and Prof. Dr. E.h. Friedhelm 
Loh. The Presidium also carries out the functions of a 
Personnel Committee and a Nomination Committee. The 
Audit Committee comprises Uwe Röhrhoff (Chairman), 
Prof. Dr. Dieter H. Vogel and Dagmar Steinert. Uwe 
Röhrhoff and Dagmar Steinert, as independent members 
of the Company’s Supervisory Board, each meet the 
requirements of a “financial expert” with expertise in the 
field of financial reporting and auditing, and were formally 
designated as such by the Supervisory Board in its 
meeting of December 11, 2024. The Audit Committee 
meets the requirements under the German Corporate 
Governance Code (hereinafter the “Code”) and Section 
100 (5) of the German Stock Corporation Act (AktG) with 
regard to the special expertise of its members. The Audit 
Committee also acts as the Sustainability Committee. In 
the latter capacity, it supports the Supervisory Board in 
monitoring the implementation of the Management 
Board’s sustainability strategy, including sustainability 
reporting. Further information on the Audit Committee 
can be found in the Corporate Governance Statement in 
10
KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

this Annual Report. At the plenary meetings, the 
committee chairpersons reported regularly and in-depth 
on topics covered in and outcomes of committee 
meetings. Where permitted by law, certain decision-
making powers have been delegated to the committees.
The Management Board is closely involved in the work of 
the Supervisory Board. All members of the Management 
Board took part in Supervisory Board meetings. The 
meetings of the Presidium were attended by the CEO and 
in some cases the CFO; those of the Audit Committee 
were generally attended by the CFO and the CEO. In the 
course of its meetings, the Supervisory Board also 
regularly consulted without the Management Board. At 
the Audit Committee meetings on March 5, 2024 and July 
29, 2024, which the auditor was asked to attend in the 
role of expert (to discuss the annual and consolidated 
financial statements and to discuss the half-year financial 
report, respectively), a resolution of the Audit Committee 
was adopted stating that the presence of the 
aforementioned Management Board members was 
deemed necessary. In addition, at the meeting on March 
5, 2024, the Audit Committee initially consulted with 
representatives of the auditor without the presence of the 
Management Board. The July 29, 2024 meeting included a 
separate agenda item for consultation with the auditor 
without the Management Board.
It is the individual responsibility of the members of the 
Supervisory Board to keep themselves informed about 
current issues and matters relating to their professional 
practice, and to further their training and professional 
development. They are supported in this by the Company, 
which in particular reimburses reasonable expenses for 
external professional development activities. Moreover, an 
onboarding program is conducted for newly elected 
Supervisory Board members. As part of this, they receive a 
comprehensive information package and the legally 
required guidance documents. They also have the 
opportunity to meet the Management Board and, where 
appropriate, individual heads of corporate departments. 
Also during the reporting year, on the occasion of the 
September meeting of the Supervisory Board, an audit 
firm provided training that was attended by all members 
of the Supervisory Board on questions of sustainability 
reporting based on the CSRD, taking into account the 
results of the materiality assessment performed for 
Klöckner. 
Meeting attendance
The Supervisory Board held a total of four plenary 
meetings in fiscal year 2024. In addition, three resolutions 
were adopted by written procedure. The Presidium met 
four times in the reporting year and the Audit Committee 
five times.
Plenary meetings were held in person, with individual 
members partly attending by video call. The same applied 
to the meetings of the Presidium in the reporting year, 
with the exception of the extraordinary meeting on March 
28, 2024, which was held as a video conference. Two 
meetings of the Audit Committee took place via video 
conference. These were the meetings to discuss the first-
quarter interim management statement and the half-year 
financial report prior to publication in each case. The 
March, October and December meetings were held in 
person.
Prof. Dr. Tobias Kollmann was absent from the 
Supervisory Board meeting on March 5, 2024 due to 
illness. Otherwise, all Supervisory Board and committee 
members attended all meetings in fiscal year 2024 
(disclosure in accordance with Code Recommendation 
D.7). The cumulative attendance rate for all Supervisory 
Board meetings, including committee meetings, was 
consequently 98%.

Supervisory Board meeting agenda items and 
resolutions
During the fiscal year under review, the Supervisory Board 
regularly addressed the business situation, strategy 
implementation and strategy development, governance, 
risk and compliance issues as well as matters pertaining to 
the Management Board and Supervisory Board. Multiple 
meetings also covered reporting on ongoing projects 
including Group financing.
The Group’s business performance in fiscal year 2024 was 
largely characterized by a decline in demand in the core 
European markets and a negative steel price trend for 
much of the reporting period. The USA in particular saw a 
significant steel price correction, although steel prices also 
developed negatively in Europe. Steel prices stabilized at a 
low level only towards the end of the fiscal year. This 
development in steel prices had a negative impact on 
Group operating income.
In addition to updates on market and business 
developments, the main topic of the Supervisory Board 
meetings was above all corporate strategy. In the 
implementation of the strategy, the sale of the country 
organizations in France, the United Kingdom, the 
Netherlands (excluding the metering business) and 
Belgium was successfully completed in the fiscal year. The 
decision was also made to invest in a new aluminum 
processing facility in the USA, in order to accelerate 
growth in the automotive and industrial sectors in the 
important growth markets of the USA and Mexico.
The Supervisory Board and the Management Board 
watched 
and 
analyzed 
share 
price 
performance 
throughout the reporting year, with a special focus at the 
extraordinary meeting of the Presidium on March 28, 
2024. During the reporting year, capital markets were 
largely influenced by slowing inflation rates and the 
associated easing of central bank policies. Market 
developments were also shaped by geopolitical events. 
Klöckner & Co’s share price followed a negative trend in 
2024 and ended the year significantly below the closing 
price from a year earlier. 
The Supervisory Board also closely monitored the 
financing side. In fiscal year 2023, following the 
acquisition of National Material of Mexico, the 
Supervisory Board thus decided to expand the financing 
arrangements to include working capital financing in 
Mexico. This was implemented during the reporting year. 
The Supervisory Board had already addressed the future 
structure of the syndicated loan and the European ABS 
program in 2023 with a view to the intended sale of the 
country organizations in France, the United Kingdom, the 
Netherlands (excluding the metering business) and 
Belgium. Following approval by the Supervisory Board, the 
syndicated loan was consequently increased in the 
reporting year. In December 2024, the Supervisory Board 
additionally approved an extension of the syndicated loan 
in an amend-and-extend process. Also during the 
reporting year, the Supervisory Board approved a renewal 
of local financing in Switzerland by switching from the 
previous bilateral lines to a syndicated credit line.
Significant topics dealt with at the Supervisory Board 
meetings included the following:
At its meeting on March 5, 2024, the Supervisory Board 
approved, among other items, the Company’s annual and 
consolidated financial statements for 2023, the Group 
12
KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

non-financial report and the dependency report for fiscal 
year 2023. Other topics discussed and resolved upon were 
Supervisory Board matters (proposal for election to the 
Supervisory Board at the 2024 Annual General Meeting; 
amendments to the Rules of Procedure for the 
Supervisory Board and for the Audit Committee with 
regard to the CSRD Directive) and Management Board 
matters 
(the 
2023 
bonus, 
amendments 
to 
the 
Management Board remuneration system). Current 
market and business developments and the corporate 
strategy were also discussed. Further topics comprised the 
engagement for the audit of the financial statements, for 
the substantive audit of the remuneration report and for 
the review of the sustainability reporting for the fiscal 
year 2024. Moreover, the Supervisory Board adopted the 
proposals for the resolutions of the Company’s 2024 
Annual General Meeting, including the proposal for 
election of the auditor for fiscal year 2024. The proposal 
to the Annual General Meeting for the election to the 
Supervisory 
Board 
and 
the 
amendment 
of 
the 
remuneration system for the Management Board had 
been discussed previously under the “Supervisory Board 
matters” and “Management Board matters” agenda items. 
In addition, the Supervisory Board approved two 
investments subject to approval at sites in Mexico, and 
the acceptance of an external board position by Guido 
Kerkhoff (as Chairman of Initiativkreis Ruhr).
The Supervisory Board meeting on May 23, 2024 was 
largely devoted to preparing for the ensuing Company’s 
Annual General Meeting, which was again held in person 
in the reporting year with the presence of shareholders. 
Current market and business developments were also 
discussed. 
The Supervisory Board meeting on September 17, 2024 
focused on business developments and corporate 
strategy. Other topics included Supervisory Board matters 
(preparation of the Supervisory Board self-assessment; 
updating of the qualification matrix) and Management 
Board matters (appraisal of the Annual General Meeting 
vote on the 2024 remuneration system for the 
Management Board; contract extensions for Dr. Oliver 
Falk and John Ganem) as well as the possibility of the 
engagement for the review of the sustainability reporting 
in the absence of national legislation implementing the 
CSRD. The Supervisory Board also approved a renewal of 
local financing in Switzerland and discussed in detail with 
the Management Board a potential investment in a new 
facility for aluminum processing in the USA.
At its meeting on December 11, 2024, the Supervisory 
Board primarily dealt with corporate planning and the 
budget for fiscal year 2025 (including the three 
subsequent years), evaluation of the Supervisory Board 
self-assessment and finalization of the qualification 
matrix, and the formal designation of the accounting and 
auditing experts. The meeting also focused on corporate 
governance matters (the Declaration of Conformity) and 
Management Board matters (the setting of bonus targets 
for fiscal year 2025 and target remuneration for fiscal year 
2025). Moreover, the annual report on governance, risk 
and compliance (GRC) topics was discussed and the 
resolution passed for the engagement for the review of 
the sustainability reporting. Finally, the Supervisory Board 
approved the extension of the syndicated loan in an 
amend-and-extend process and the acceptance of an 
external mandate by Mr. Ganem.
In addition, three resolutions were adopted by written 
procedure in the reporting year: On January 15, 2024 for 
approval of the increase in the syndicated loan, on March 
15, 2024 for the engagement of the auditor for the 2024 
financial statements and the substantive audit of the 
remuneration report, and on October 4, 2024 for the final 
approval of the investment in a new aluminum processing 
facility in the USA.
Reports from the committees
Presidium:
The Presidium met a total of four times in 2024. The 
standard agenda items in all ordinary meetings of the 
Presidium comprised business development, an update on 
strategy and the agenda for the following Supervisory 
Board meeting.

Audit of the 2024 annual and consolidated financial 
statements including the dependency report and the

Klöckner & Co SE’s annual financial statements for fiscal 
year 2024 and the consolidated financial statements and 
combined management report were audited and issued 
with an unqualified audit opinion by PricewaterhouseCoopers 
GmbH Wirtschaftsprüfungsgesellschaft, Düsseldorf, the 
auditor elected by the Annual General Meeting and 
engaged by the Supervisory Board. Klöckner & Co SE’s 
annual 
financial

and

combined

were prepared in accordance with German commercial 
law. Pursuant to Section 315e of the German Commercial 
Code (HGB), the consolidated financial statements were 
prepared in accordance with International Financial 
Reporting Standards (IFRS) as endorsed by the European 
Union. The audit reports and further documentation 
relating to the financial statements, including the Group 
non-financial report and the audit report thereon, were 
made available to all members of the Supervisory Board in 
a timely manner; the same applies to the dependency 
report and the audit report on that. These documents were 
dealt with in detail by both the Audit Committee and the 
plenary Supervisory Board. In particular, in relation to the 
annual and consolidated financial statements, the key 
audit matters described in the relevant audit opinion as 
well as the audit procedures applied were also discussed. 
The auditor took part in the discussions, reported on the 
material findings of their audit and responded to 
questions. The Management Board was also represented at 
these meetings (following a corresponding resolution by 
the Supervisory Board or the Audit Committee, as 
applicable). In the course of its meeting, the Audit 
Committee also consulted with the auditor without the 
Management Board (in particular on the annual and 
consolidated financial statements). At the Supervisory 
Board meeting held on March 5, 2025 to approve the 
annual financial statements, the Chairman of the Audit 
Committee reported on the Audit Committee’s consultations 
on the annual and consolidated financial statements and 
the combined management report. With regard to the risk 
early warning system, the auditor stated that the 
Management Board had taken the measures required in 
Section 91 (2) of the German Stock Corporation Act in an 
appropriate manner – in particular for establishing a 
monitoring system – and that the monitoring system was 
capable of promptly identifying developments threatening 
the Company’s ability to continue as a going concern. The 
same applies with regard to the internal control system 
and risk management system (in each case including the 
compliance management system) to be implemented in 
accordance with Section 91 (3) of the German Stock 
Corporation Act. The Supervisory Board noted with 
approval the results of the audit by the auditor and the 
explanations provided by the Chairman of the Audit 
Committee and, on completion of its own examination of 
the 
Company’s 
annual 
financial 
statements, 
the 
consolidated financial statements and the combined 
management report – as well as in line with the Audit 
Committee’s recommendation – did not raise any objections.

financial statements and consolidated financial statements 
prepared by the Management Board, the annual financial 
statements are adopted.

SHARE PRICE PERFORMANCE 
In 2024, global equity markets were marked by declining inflation rates, accompanied by adjustments to central banks' 
monetary policy strategies. Geopolitical tensions and disruptions in global supply chains exacerbated the uncertainties and 
challenges on the markets. In the first few months of 2024, the price of the Klöckner & Co share largely moved sideways. The 
high of €7.01 was reached on February 12, 2024. As the year progressed, the share price declined considerably, reaching a low 
of €4.41 on November 13. The stock was subsequently able to recover somewhat, but then fell again and closed on 
December 30, 2024, at a price of €4.44.

KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

PERFORMANCE OF KLÖCKNER & CO SHARES COMPARED TO THE DAX, SDAX AND INDEX PEER GROUP (INDEXED VALUES)
Over fiscal year 2024, the Klöckner & Co share price lost around 35% on the prior-year closing price. The Klöckner & Co share 
is benchmarked against a peer group index, which lost approximately 29% in the reporting period. This index tracks the share 
price performance of companies that are comparable to Klöckner & Co and is based on thyssenkrupp, Salzgitter, ArcelorMittal, 
Voestalpine, Swiss Steel, Reliance, Olympic Steel, Ryerson and Worthington Steel. The German benchmark index DAX® rose 
by 19% compared to the previous year, while the SDAX® index lost almost 2%. In Deutsche Börse AG’s December 2024 joint 
rankings of DAX®, MDAX® and SDAX® stocks covering a total of 160 companies, Klöckner & Co shares ranked 149th by free 
float market capitalization. 
MARKET CAPITALIZATION
The Company’s market capitalization was approximately €443 million at the end of the reporting period, compared to 
€685 million as of December 29, 2023.

ANNUAL GENERAL MEETING 2024
The 18th Annual General Meeting of Klöckner & Co SE was held in person in Düsseldorf on May 23, 2024. In total, more than 
61% of the voting capital voted on resolutions. All resolutions proposed by the Supervisory Board and Management Board 
were approved by a clear majority of the shareholders. 
Klöckner & Co once again made an online tool available to shareholders in the run-up to the Annual General Meeting. 
Shareholders were able to register for the Annual General Meeting on our website at https://www.kloeckner.com. Using the 
online service, it was easy to order an admission ticket, submit authorizations and instructions for proxy holders and order 
postal voting documents. The tool also allows shareholders to request the invitation to the Annual General Meeting 
electronically through the e-mail service (electronic delivery). For registered users, this replaces delivery by postal mail.
All shareholders were able to follow a livestream of the entire meeting via the online service on the Klöckner & Co SE website, 
https://www.kloeckner.com. In addition, the Annual General Meeting, up to the general debate, was livestreamed on the 
website for the public. The speeches by Supervisory Board Chairman Prof. Dr. Dieter H. Vogel and CEO Guido Kerkhoff remain 
available there for viewing. 
GROUP OF ANALYSTS
As in previous years, analysts continued to show strong interest in Klöckner & Co stock in 2024. During the reporting period, 
six analysts followed the development of the stock and published a large number of research reports. At the end of the year, 
five securities firms issued a buy recommendation, while another advised holding the share. No sell recommendation was 
made. Current analyst ratings can be viewed at any time in the “Investors/Stock/Analysts” section of our company website.

The investor relations team once again commissioned regular analyses in 2024 in order to ascertain the ownership structure

instruments).

1.
Fundamental information about the Group
The statements marked in the management report with these parentheses [ ] and the website links are unaudited voluntary content 
that has been critically read by the auditor.
1.1  
Group structure
The Group is divided into two operating segments: Kloeckner Metals Americas and Kloeckner Metals Europe. Headquarters 
functions not allocated to a segment are reported separately, together with consolidation adjustments, under Holding and 
other group companies.
Klöckner & Co SE’s subscribed share capital remains unchanged at a total of €249.38 million, composed of 99.75 million no-
par-value registered shares carrying full voting rights. Since the initial public offering at the end of June 2006, Klöckner & Co 
SE’s shares have been listed on the Frankfurt Stock Exchange’s Regulated Market (Prime Standard).
The sale of our distribution business in France, the United Kingdom, the Netherlands and Belgium was successfully completed 
in the reporting period. In accordance with IFRS 5, the disposal group was already presented as “discontinued operations” in 
the prior year. For discontinued operations, for both the prior year and the fiscal year under review, all income and expenses 
are presented separately in the income statement and all cash flows are presented separately in the statement of cash flows 
until disposal. Deconsolidation took place effective February 29, 2024. 
26
KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

1.2  
Business activities/business model
Klöckner & Co is one of the [largest producer-independent] distributors of steel and metal products and one of the world’s 
leading steel service center companies. We act as a connecting link between steel producers and consumers. As we are not 
affiliated with any particular steel producer, our customers benefit from our wide range of national and international sourcing 
options spanning some 40 main suppliers worldwide. Our key competitive advantages include economies of scale in global 
procurement, our broad product portfolio and customer access via an extensive sales and distribution network, in addition to a 
very wide range of processing services. Klöckner & Co plans to further expand its processing portfolio in order to benefit from 
the higher-margin processing business and from being significantly less dependent on steel price trends. With a focus on the 
“DACH” region (Germany, Austria and Switzerland) and North America, our global network provides customers with local 
access to some 120 distribution and service locations. The high level of availability of our roughly 165,000 products largely 
eliminates the need for our customers to hold inventory. Our customer base comprises more than 60,000 mostly small to 
medium-sized steel and metal consumers, primarily from the construction industry, machinery and mechanical engineering, 
the transportation industry and other metalworking companies.
We provide customers with an optimized, end-to-end solution from procurement through logistics to prefabrication, including 
individual delivery and 24-hour service – processes we are increasingly digitalizing and automating. For example, we employ a 
variety of digital applications and tools that provide our customers and business partners with access to a broader spectrum of 
steel and metal products as well as services. We are constantly developing this digital portfolio in partnership with our 
customers. In addition, we see significant opportunities in the transformation toward sustainable business models and already 
offer our customers CO2-reduced solutions with regard to materials, processing, logistics and circularity solutions together 
with comprehensive advice in order to help customers build a sustainable value chain. 
Our approximately 6,500 employees apply their skills and enthusiasm every day to meeting our customers’ needs and wishes. 
Around 52% of our workforce is employed in Europe and 48% in the Americas.
Both in North America and Europe, the market for warehouse-based distribution and steel service centers is highly fragmented. 
We have an estimated market share of approximately 9% in Europe and around 7% in the USA, putting us among the [top four 
distributors and top three steel service centers] in both regions.

1.3 
Corporate strategy 
[Our strategy – “Klöckner & Co: Leveraging Strengths”
In implementing our “Klöckner & Co: Leveraging Strengths” strategy, we focus primarily on four levers: Customer Growth, 
Leveraging Assets & Partner Network, Digitalization & Value Chain Automation, and Operational Excellence.
We aim to generate added value for all of our Company’s stakeholders. And we want customers and business partners to 
benefit from seamlessly integrated, digitalized and automated processes. For employees, we aim to foster a culture of 
empowerment and collaboration, upskilling them for the future and enabling them to grow and develop. For shareholders, our 
focus on a higher level of profitability also means a focus on the sustainable financial success of their investment in 
Klöckner & Co. Furthermore, we strive to make a positive impact on society and the environment.
We aim to establish Klöckner & Co as the leading one-stop shop for steel and metal products as well as higher value-added 
processing in North America and Europe. In addition, we want to bring the digital and the physical sides of our business closer 
together and to continuously improve our internal and external networks. The acquisition of Mexican service center National 
Material of Mexico and of the US metal components manufacturer Industrial Manufacturing Services, as well as the sale of 
parts of our European distribution business, further strengthen our focus on higher value-added business and reduce our 
exposure to volatile commodity markets. Inefficiencies in low-margin steel and metal distribution are still primarily caused by 
linear supply chains and lack of transparency. By integrating third parties while both digitalizing and automating core 
processes, we can eliminate existing inefficiencies in the value chain and significantly reduce our variable costs.
28
KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

Customer Growth
Our goal is to deliver the best solutions for our customers and add value in everything we do. By focusing uncompromisingly 
on their needs, we aim to achieve the highest customer satisfaction in the industry. This requires an extension of our product 
and service portfolio and larger regional coverage. The successful acquisition of National Material of Mexico has allowed us to 
expand our business in North America and increase our presence in Mexico, which is an important region for automotive and 
industrial customers. As a one-stop shop with a fast-track, best-in-class user experience, we aim to grow our client base and 
increase our share of wallet: Customers will buy more from us, and more customers will buy from us.
Leveraging Assets and Partner Network
To improve efficiency, we aim to integrate our partners more deeply into internal processes along the value chain while 
optimizing network and asset utilization through increased international collaboration. We also plan to bring on board new 
partners with complementary product ranges and competencies outside Klöckner & Co’s core portfolio.
Digitalization and Value Chain Automation
Building on our pioneering role in the steel industry, we are further exploiting the significant potential of digitalization and 
extending it to the level of automation. We develop innovative solutions and continue to digitalize our internal core processes. 
With seamless, end-to-end process integration featuring a very high degree of digitalization and automation, we can take 
process speed and efficiency to unparalleled levels along the entire value chain. Our goal is “zero touch,” meaning value 
generation with minimum manual effort. In the reporting period, we improved the efficiency of our digital ordering processes 
in the relevant organizational units. As a result, the number of digital quotes was increased by more than 26% compared to the 
prior-year period. 
Operational Excellence
In order to deliver the most efficient solutions and best service to our customers, we are continuously improving our 
operations. Excellence is our benchmark. By eliminating inefficiencies in our internal processes, we are optimizing our 
operational results and increasing profitability. We continue to further advance excellence in operations and sales through 
process automation, increased cost and process transparency, data-driven decision-making and organizational measures.
Corporate Values
Our values of Collaboration, Excellence and Responsibility are the cornerstones of our corporate culture and the foundation of 
our success. We live these values every day; they shape the way we think and the way we work together as a team and with 
our customers. 
We are committed to responsibility for the environment, safety and our community. This commitment must be reflected in all 
our actions and decisions. By enforcing the highest safety standards, we ensure a safe working environment that protects our 
employees. In terms of integrity, we are a transparent, authentic and modern company and deliver on our promises. We 
demonstrate accountability and commitment to our decisions and actions, and we foster a culture of dealing openly with 
failures. Our “purpose” – the reason for our existence or the description of what we do to create value for our stakeholders – 
describes what unites us at Klöckner and demonstrates our positive role in society. 
“We partner with customers and suppliers to deliver 
innovative metal solutions for a sustainable tomorrow.”
With the initiatives that make up our “Klöckner & Co: Leveraging Strengths” strategy, we continue to merge the digital and 
physical sides of our business and take them to the next level. Klöckner & Co will thus become the leading one-stop shop for 
steel and metal products as well as higher value-added processing in North America and Europe.

Sustainability strategy
We view our dedicated sustainability strategy from an overarching environmental, social and governance (ESG) perspective 
and purposefully integrate that perspective. Social responsibility and reliable corporate governance are integral elements here 
alongside environmental aspects.
We believe that innovation, technology and new business models, in particular, will enable the steel and metal industry’s 
successful transformation to sustainability. As part of our Group strategy, we are consequently working as a pioneer of a 
sustainable steel industry to establish innovative business models by creating a comprehensive portfolio of sustainable 
customer solutions. By expanding our product and service portfolio, we are seizing the strategic opportunity to integrate the 
attractive new business area of sustainable solutions into our business model. We see this transformation as a unique growth 
opportunity – not just in the future, but right now today.
    
Under our Nexigen® umbrella brand, we have focused our sustainable product and service portfolio across the Group, 
providing transparent, CO2-reduced solutions in the categories of materials, processing, logistics, circularity (closed-loop) 
solutions and comprehensive Sustainability Advisory Services for sustainable customer solutions. In this way, we are already 
helping customers to source CO2-reduced steel and metal products, while our smart software solutions give them visibility into 
the carbon footprint of the products they buy. To provide our customers with optimum support in establishing sustainable 
value chains, we have introduced rating scales for our CO2-reduced steel, stainless steel and aluminum products. The 
calculation methodology is rooted in international, science-based standards and categorizes the CO2-reduced materials 
according to their certifiably calculated emissions along the entire value chain from resource extraction to production and 
processing, or cradle to Kloeckner exit gate. Our scales serve customers as a guide and a comparison tool for determining the 
carbon footprint of end products. Through partnerships, we already offer our customers CO2-reduced steel and metal products 
today.
30
KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

Categorization for carbon steel, in kg CO2e per ton of steel.
In addition, we provide customers with an individual product carbon footprint (PCF) for almost every item in our product 
portfolio. This allows customers to reliably, transparently and easily verify the carbon footprint of a product purchased from 
Klöckner & Co. With the Nexigen® PCF Algorithm, we have developed an innovative tool whose automated PCF calculation 
methodology is certified by TÜV SÜD. The Nexigen® PCF Algorithm’s methodology for calculating the PCF follows the 
internationally recognized Greenhouse Gas Protocol and ISO 14067, ref. 14040 and 14044, and, in accordance with those 
standards, calculates the product’s cradle-to-customer entry gate emissions. As a result, customers have information about the 
carbon footprint of their materials, thus enabling them to make more sustainable product decisions.

1.4 
Control system
Financial performance indicators
The most significant key performance indicators (KPIs) used in the management of Klöckner & Co’s business in the year under 
review were shipments, sales, operating income (EBITDA – earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization and 
impairments and impairment reversals on intangible assets and property, plant and equipment) and cash flow from operating 
activities. These central KPIs were reported and monitored at the level of the Group as a whole as well as at segmental level. 
Shipments are a key performance indicator used in management of the distribution business. This indicator is used to monitor 
growth in the core business as well as to determine capacity utilization, which is important for planning personnel and machine 
resources. Under our strategy, we continue to build our business model to become the leading one-stop shop for our 
customers. In line with this, our sales are expected to reach a higher level in the medium term, with reduced volatility. We 
therefore constantly monitor our sales and margin growth. The most significant KPI for results of operations is operating 
income (EBITDA), or, if there are material special effects, EBITDA before material special effects. In order to increase its 
information value as an indicator of the sustainable operating earning power of the business, EBITDA is adjusted for certain 
material special effects. Special effects are effects that are not attributable to the operating business and/or relate to other 
periods. They include income and expenses from the sale of real estate and investments, income and expenses for 
restructuring, and other income and expenses that by nature are not attributable to the current operating business. The 
indicator makes it possible to present earnings from the operating business and allows better comparison with the 
corresponding earnings of other companies. The reconciliation of EBITDA before material special effects to EBITDA including 
material special effects is presented under “Results of operations, financial position and net assets.”
Fixed asset intensity ratios tend to be low in steel distribution, while net working capital (sum of inventories plus trade 
receivables less trade payables) tends to be very high. Alongside operating income, net working capital is the primary driver of 
cash flow from operating activities. This cash flow thus forms an objective basis for measuring the performance of our business 
activities.
In addition to these primary key performance indicators, we also monitor other important KPIs. Gross profit is sales less cost of 
goods sold and is thus an indicator of the Company’s value creation. In view of the time lag between the setting of 
procurement and selling prices, we closely monitor price trends in procurement markets as a supporting measure. Return on 
capital employed (ROCE) is calculated as earnings before interest and taxes (EBIT) divided by the average capital employed. 
Net financial debt (financial liabilities less cash and cash equivalents) is an important indicator in the financial management of 
the Company. Changes in net financial debt also reflect cash generated by the business. Capital markets also take net financial 
debt into account valuing our Company. For that reason, we constantly monitor gearing (net financial debt/equity), equity and 
the leverage ratio (net financial debt/EBITDA).
These key performance indicators are the basis of management processes and decision making at strategic and operating level, 
including for purposes such as investment and acquisition decisions. Changes in the key performance indicators are reported on 
in the “Results of operations, financial position and net assets” section.

Non-financial performance indicators
We believe that non-financial targets likewise support the Company’s success. Accordingly, we have adopted suitable 
initiatives to improve workplace safety and health in our Company; one example is the Group-wide Safety 1st program. The 
measures aim to ensure safe working conditions as well as to reduce accidents at work and the costs they entail. Our key 
performance indicator for this purpose is the lost time injury frequency (LTIF). This is defined as the number of accidents/
number of hours worked x 1,000,000. 
On the way to a sustainable steel industry, Klöckner & Co assumes social responsibility while at the same time exploiting the 
strategic opportunities offered by decarbonization. Our net zero carbon targets have been recognized by the Science Based 
Targets initiative (SBTi) as science-based targets in the standard validation process. The Group consequently aligns its business 
activities with science-based targets in order to contribute to limiting global warming to 1.5°C through appropriate measures. 
Contributing to the achievement of this global target, Klöckner & Co aims for a 62.5% reduction in its directly controllable 
emissions (Scope 1 and 2) by 2030 compared to the 2019 baseline. The carbon emissions for the 2019 base year and the 
medium-term carbon reduction target for Scope 1 and 2 have been adjusted to reflect changes in the corporate structure. 
These adjustments ensure that the data remains comparable from year to year (see also the information in the Group non-
financial report).
Digitalization and automation will continue to be a basis for the growth and long-term profitability of the Company as we 
strive to increase the transparency and efficiency of our supply chains. In 2024, we defined three corporate goals for 
digitalization. By means of our Kloeckner Assistant and the use of online shops and contract platforms, we aim to continuously 
increase the digital orders received by digital channels in total orders and the share of digital quotations in total quotations for 
customers. As a further measure of success in the digital transformation of our business, we have selected the efficiency of the 
digital ordering processes, defined as the number of manual corrections to orders received via digital channels as a percentage 
of the total number of digital orders. 
Employee satisfaction is a further factor in our business success. At a time when there is a shortage of skilled labor, it helps us 
retain employees for the long term, which positively impacts the performance and productivity of our business processes. For 
this reason, we measure employee satisfaction as part of our annual Group-wide employee survey and infer specific 
improvement measures where necessary.

KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

2. Economic report
2.1 
Macroeconomic conditions
Economic environment
Macroeconomic situation
According to the International Monetary Fund (IMF), the global economy grew slightly by 3.2% during the reporting period, 
with significant regional differences. While economic growth in the US was stronger than initially assumed, the developed 
economies in Europe tended to be weaker than expected. In many countries, structural problems such as an aging population 
and low productivity prevented further economic growth. Central bank interest rate hikes also led to a gradual decline in global 
inflation, but the economic challenges remained: Inflation in the services sector continued to exert upward pressure in many 
regions, while prices for goods largely stabilized. 
According to the IMF, the US economy grew slightly by 2.8% year-on-year in 2024. Economic growth was mainly supported by 
robust private consumption as a result of real wage increases and infrastructure investment. Government infrastructure 
programs have strengthened the American economy by encouraging production shifts and attracting additional investment. 
The period under review saw the Federal Reserve cut key interest rates for the first time since 2020. Based on falling inflation 
and the first signs of a slowdown in labor demand, the key rate was lowered by a total of one percentage point, leading to an 
increase in investment and a stabilization of the economy. Reduced uncertainty following the outcome of the presidential 
election has already contributed to a short-run improvement in manufacturing sentiment. 
According to the IMF, the eurozone economy remained constant in 2024 (with an increase of 0.8%) compared to the prior 
year. The economic growth was mainly driven by improved exports, but investment declined noticeably in the first half of the 
year, partly due to the high level of economic uncertainty. Despite an increase in disposable incomes, households maintained a 
high savings rate, thus curbing consumption. Towards the end of the period, a gradual return of consumer confidence and 
monetary policy easing by the ECB finally led to a slight recovery in domestic demand, but not enough to offset the inhibiting 
factors. 
Despite continued weakness in the real estate sector and deteriorating consumer confidence, China’s economic growth slowed 
only slightly to 4.8% in 2024, mainly due to better-than-expected net exports. The Chinese government implemented an 
economic stimulus package in the year under review, issuing special government bonds to support low-income households, 
stabilize the ailing real estate market, and strengthen the capital base of state-owned banks.

Industry-specific situation

Union recorded slight growth of 2.6%. In China, crude steel production in 2024 was slightly lower than in the previous year

2.2 
Sector environment
Klöckner & Co’s highest-revenue customer sector in fiscal year 2024 was the construction industry, accounting for 28% of 
sales, followed by manufacturing and machinery and mechanical engineering with 25%. The third-largest customer sector was 
the automotive industry as part of the transportation industry, which accounted in total for 21% of sales.

2.3 
Trend in key customer industries
Construction industry
According to Oxford Economics, the US construction industry grew slightly (by 4%) in 2024, driven by strong growth in the 
nonresidential and civil engineering sectors. Residential construction showed a slight decrease of 2%. The main growth driver 
for the US construction industry was strong public investment as a result of the Inflation Reduction Act and improved 
borrowing conditions. Persistently high material costs prevented growth from being even stronger. The eurozone construction 
industry remained constant in 2024 (0%), while in Switzerland the sector experienced a slight decline (by 1%). A decline in 
residential construction was largely offset in the period under review by strong growth in civil engineering, which benefited 
above all from investment in sustainable construction projects and lower ECB key interest rates. The Swiss National Bank also 
further lowered its key interest rate during the period. However, the industry continued to suffer from a structural labour 
shortage. 
Machinery and mechanical engineering
According to estimates by Oxford Economics, machinery and mechanical engineering in the United States showed a constant 
trend in the reporting period (0%) compared to the prior year. Monetary policy easing already had a positive short-term 
impact during the period under review, supporting investment and helping to offset the weakness in aggregate demand caused 
by high interest rates. In the eurozone, the sector contracted slightly (by 4%). Positive effects from looser ECB monetary policy 
were unable to offset the overall weakened ability to invest due to low consumer confidence and a further deterioration of the 
outlook for industry as a whole.
Automotive industry
The global automotive market recorded slight growth (of 1%) in 2024. According to the German Association of the 
Automotive Industry (VDA), automobile sales in the USA also increased slightly by 2% in the period under review. In Mexico, 
automobile sales recorded considerable growth of around 10%. Automobile sales in Europe increased slightly by around 1% 
year on year, but remained visibly below the level seen in 2019. Persistent uncertainty about the future of conventionally 
powered cars, low consumer confidence and ongoing restraint in consumer spending held back growth in this sector.

2.4 
Comparison of the Group’s actual business performance with the forecast from the prior year
Our forecast for 2024 was based on the assumption that inflation would slow down and economic conditions in the sales 
markets relevant to us in North America and Europe would gradually return to normal, with demand picking up as a result. 
Looking back over the year, macroeconomic conditions remained challenging overall, particularly in Europe. In addition, steel 
prices underwent a marked correction over much of the reporting period, with steel prices falling especially in the US but also 
in Europe. 
Contrary to our initial expectations, demand for steel and other metals in the Kloeckner Metals Europe segment remained 
muted due to the continued challenging macroeconomic environment in Europe and did not show the expected increase. As 
expected, the Kloeckner Metals Americas segment was able to report a considerable increase, while the Group as a whole saw 
a slight increase. 
In line with the forecast increase in shipments in both segments and at Group level, we also expected considerably higher sales. 
Contrary to our forecast, sales in the Kloeckner Metals Europe segment showed a considerable decrease. The Kloeckner Metals 
Americas segment achieved a slight increase. In contrast to our forecast, sales also declined slightly at Group level. The decline 
in sales is mainly due to the steel price correction over large parts of the reporting period.
With regard to EBITDA before material special effects, we forecast a considerable increase for the year under review in both 
segments and at Group level. As the macroeconomic environment remained challenging, particularly in Europe, and steel 
prices declined, EBITDA before material special effects did not increase as we originally expected and was considerably down 
on the prior year. 
Following cash flow from operating activities of €287 million in fiscal year 2023, we expected another clearly positive cash 
flow from operating activities for fiscal year 2024, although considerably below the level of the previous year. At segment 
level, the Kloeckner Metals Americas segment showed a considerable decline as forecast, while the Kloeckner Metals Europe 
segment saw a considerable increase as forecast. At Group-level, cash flow from operating activities amounted to €160 million, 
which as forecast was considerably positive but below the prior-year level.

Shipments and sales
Shipments increased slightly year on year in fiscal year 2024 to 4.5 million tons. The increase in shipments (of 4.8%) compared 
to the prior year is due in particular to the acquisitions made in the USA and Mexico in the second half of 2023. In Europe, the 
continued challenging macroeconomic environment led a downward trend in demand.
Due to lower prices, sales fell from €7.0 billion to €6.6 billion, marking a slight decrease of 4.7% on the prior year. The USA in 
particular saw a significant steel price correction in 2024, although steel prices also developed negatively in Europe. Overall, 
average price levels during the year was thus considerably below the prior-year level in both operating segments and at Group 
level. The currency-adjusted decline in Group sales was 4.9%.

Comparability of OPEX with the prior year is limited due to special effects. Other operating income, at €38 million, was 
€4 million higher than in the prior year. This is mainly due to non-recurring income from insurance settlements of €6 million 
for damage caused by Hurricane Helene at a site in the USA and to the sale of assets at a discontinued site in the Kloeckner 
Metals Americas segment.
Personnel expense amounted to €542 million in the reporting period, compared to €535 million in the prior year. The increase 
is mainly due to higher wage and salary expenses, which were only partially offset by savings in earnings-based salary 
components. On a currency-adjusted basis, personnel expenses came to €540 million (+1.1%).
Other operating expenses increased from €466 million to €499 million. On a currency-adjusted basis, other operating 
expenses amounted to €500 million (+7.4%). The increase is mainly due to higher costs of maintenance, transportation and 
packaging and to higher prices of operating supplies and tools.
In total, at €109 million, EBITDA after material special effects was considerably below the prior-year figure of €190 million.

Financial position, financing and liquidity
Financing and financial management
Group financing is centrally managed through Klöckner & Co SE. We secure the liquidity of our Group companies in intra-
Group liquidity balancing arrangements with central and bilateral credit facilities. Cash pooling systems are used within the 
various currency areas. Centralized management of financing strengthens our negotiating position with banks and other 
lenders, making it easier to implement a uniform finance policy and limit financing risk.
We meet the challenges of the financing environment with our diversified financing structure. The current environment is 
shaped by the persistently high interest rate level in all markets relevant to Klöckner & Co, the general economic environment 
and the global conflicts and crises. We have ample financial flexibility, which we maintain due to our business model. With a 
portfolio totaling some €1.6 billion (excluding leasing), we are very solidly positioned, including with regard to contract terms 
and financial covenants. 
Financing for the Group is secured using a portfolio of funding instruments comprising an ABS program (Germany), two 
syndicated loans (Klöckner & Co SE and since January 2025 in Switzerland), two asset-based lending facilities (ABL USA and 
ABL Mexico) and bilateral loan agreements.
Syndicated loan
A central component of Group financing is our syndicated loan (a revolving credit facility) with a facility amount of 
€400 million as of the reporting date.
After the facility amount of the syndicated loan was increased from €250 million to €400 million in February 2024, we 
renewed the facility ahead of schedule in December 2024. In an amend and extend process, we adjusted the facility amount 
from €400 million to €350 million and extended it ahead of schedule to January 2028. The amendments became effective in 
January 2025. By doing so, Klöckner & Co improved the maturity profile of Group finances.
The facility is provided by a syndicate of seven banks. Under the financial covenants, gearing – defined as net financial debt 
divided by the book value of equity less non-controlling interests and less goodwill resulting from acquisitions after May 23, 
2024 – may not exceed 165%. Hence, the adjusted book value of equity may not fall below €600 million (minimum equity). 
Breach of the financial covenants would require repayment of all outstanding amounts. Subsequent drawings would then be 
possible if the covenants were once again complied with. The financial covenants were complied with in the reporting year.

ASSET-BACKED SECURITIZATION PROGRAM
Since July 2005, the Klöckner & Co Group has operated a European ABS program. The size of the program was increased in 
April 2022 from €220 million to €300 million. In December 2022, Klöckner & Co secured an extension of the program ahead 
of term. The term of the facility ends in January 2026. The agreed financial covenants are also based on the statement of 
financial position and the covenant levels are equivalent to those for the syndicated loan. Utilization of the program totaled 
€30 million as of the reporting date. The financial covenants were complied with in the reporting year.
ASSET-BASED LENDING
The borrowing base facility (ABL facility) at the US country organization was originally agreed in November 2020 and was 
increased in March 2022 from USD 330 million to USD 450 million. In July 2022, the facility was renewed ahead of schedule 
on improved terms and with a five-year duration to July 2027. In connection with the acquisition of National Material of 
Mexico, the facility was increased in December 2022 from USD 450 million to USD 650 million (approximately €626 million) 
with the same terms and maturity while expanding the banking syndicate from three to four banks. Utilization of the facility 
amounted to USD 390 million (approximately €376 million) as of the reporting date.
In September 2024, Klöckner & Co agreed a new USD 115 million (approximately €111 million) ABL facility in Mexico. The 
facility is provided by three banks and has a term of three years until September 2027. Utilization of the facility amounted to 
USD 50 million (approximately €48 million) as of the reporting date.
BILATERAL CREDIT FACILITIES
The bilateral credit facilities totaling approximately €210 million were drawn in the amount of €125 million (excluding lease 
liabilities) at the end of 2024.
The largest portion of our bilateral credit lines is accounted for by the Swiss country organization at CHF 160 million 
(approximately €170 million), with drawings of CHF 80 million (approximately €85 million) as of the reporting date. These 
revolving credit lines with three banks were most recently renewed in July 2021 and extended through to March 2025. In 
January 2025, Klöckner & Co terminated these credit lines in agreement with the core banks and established an unsecured 
syndicated loan with a volume of CHF 200 million (approximately €212 million) as a new financing instrument. In this 
connection, the bank syndicate was expanded from three to four banks. The facility has a term of four years until January 2029.
There are also other bilateral borrowings at subsidiaries, used among other things to finance net working capital or for capital 
expenditure.
LIQUIDITY MANAGEMENT AND INTER-COMPANY SETTLEMENTS
Intragroup loans and cash-pooling systems in the various currency areas are used to handle inter-company settlements and 
manage liquidity. Financing of our Group companies, including working capital for the operating business at the individual 
country organizations, was secure at all times throughout 2024.

We safeguard liquidity through rigorous inventory and receivables management as well as by adhering to internally defined 
indicators. Financial risk management is governed by Group-wide financial guidelines. We use derivative financial instruments 
to hedge interest-rate, currency and market risk. Derivatives are used exclusively to hedge risk related to underlying 
transactions. Foreign currency exposure at Group companies is generally hedged against currency risk at corporate level, or, 
where applicable, via local forex trading lines with banks that have impeccable credit ratings. We also centrally monitor and 
hedge any interest-rate risk.

KLÖCKNER & CO SE ANNUAL REPORT 2024

2.6 
Overall assessment of the business situation
The Klöckner & Co Group’s operating income (EBITDA) was significantly positive in the 2024 reporting year despite the 
macroeconomic challenges. The USA in particular saw a significant steel price correction in 2024, although steel prices also 
developed negatively in Europe. Adverse price effects as a result of this development in steel prices had a negative impact on 
Group operating income and led to an overall reduction in gross profit for the full fiscal year 2024 compared to the prior year. 
This contrasted with a slight improvement in the gross profit margin. Due to the challenges described, a net loss from 
continuing operations of €146 million was generated in the fiscal year.
Cash flow from operating activities was significantly positive in the fiscal year, mainly due to the operating income and 
consistent net working capital management.
Our finances remain very stable, including in times of a challenging macroeconomic environment. Financing for the Group is 
based on a widely diversified portfolio of funding instruments. We were able to maintain and even expand our large financial 
headroom in the fiscal year by extending financing arrangements ahead of term. The equity ratio remains very solid at 
approximately 49% at the end of the reporting period.

The annual financial statements of Klöckner & Co SE are prepared in accordance with the German Commercial Code (HGB) 
and the German Stock Corporation Act.
Klöckner & Co SE’s financial position reflects its status as holding company and its function as the Group’s central financing 
company. The opportunities and risks of Klöckner & Co SE correspond to those of the Group (see the “Risk and opportunities” 
section) and primarily impact the carrying amounts of investments and future dividend payout potential. The Company’s fixed 
assets consist almost entirely of financial assets. These mostly comprise the investments in management companies heading 
the Group’s national and international country organizations and investments in individual country operating organizations.
The development of financial assets as of December 31, 2024 is marked by the sale of a large part of the European distribution 
business. Prior to that, ODS B.V., Rotterdam, The Netherlands, made a distribution of €21 million to Klöckner & Co SE and then 
Klöckner & Co SE acquired ODS Metering Systems B.V., Rotterdam, The Netherlands for a purchase price of €23 million, 
because this line of business was not part of the sale. The sale of the European distribution business in France, the United 
Kingdom, the Netherlands and Belgium, which was written off in full in fiscal year 2023, was then completed effective 
February 29, 2024 at a final purchase price of €85 million, resulting in an additional loss of €11 million in the fiscal year that 
was accounted for in other operating expenses.

and Section 51, sentence 1 of the German SE Implementation Act (SEAG), read in conjunction with Section 19 (2), sentence 2 
of the Klöckner & Co SE Articles of Association, amendments may be adopted by a simple majority of votes cast if at least one

Company’s business purpose, resolutions on cross-border relocation of the Company’s seat and cases in which a larger capital 
majority is mandatorily required by law. For resolutions that require a three-quarter capital majority under the German Stock 
Corporation Act, a three-quarter majority of share capital represented is consequently also necessary at Klöckner & Co SE.
Under Section 21 of the Articles of Association, the Supervisory Board is authorized to make certain formal changes to the 
Articles of Association itself as and when required.

5. Macroeconomic outlook including key 
opportunities and risks
5.1 
Expected global economic growth
The International Monetary Fund (IMF) expects that the global economy will grow slightly by 3.3% in 2025. A further decline 
in inflation is expected, although disruptions are possible. Political uncertainties and geopolitical tensions pose significant risks 
to the global economy, as they can lead to fluctuations in commodity prices. Such fluctuations pose a threat to disinflation and 
could prevent central banks from easing their monetary policy. These risks are particularly evident in the development of the 
Western European economy, which could be significantly affected by a potential escalation of the war in Ukraine. Trade 
tensions, such as the European Union’s punitive tariffs on Chinese automobile imports or the new US administration’s planned 
tariffs on imports from China and other major trading partners, could also negatively impact the economy. Newly elected 
governments could also make significant changes to trade and fiscal policies that might threaten long-term growth and supply 
chain resilience. To prevent excessive increases in real interest rates, key interest rates are likely to track the decline in inflation 
and gradually fall to levels consistent with the inflation target.
For the USA, the IMF forecasts economic output to grow slightly by 2.7% in 2025. The new US administration is expected in 
general to strengthen consumer and business confidence and provide positive overall impetus to the economy. There are 
uncertainties, however, particularly with the planned introduction of general tariffs on Chinese imports and targeted tariffs on 
goods from other trading partners. These pose a risk to growth and could lead to retaliatory tariffs, which would have a 
negative impact on the development of the economy. Overall, the expectation is that a soft landing can be achieved and that 
US inflation will return to the Federal Reserve’s target level without triggering a recession. 
In the eurozone, slight growth of 1.0% is expected in 2025, supported mainly by stronger domestic demand. The economy 
stands to benefit from an expected recovery in consumption and continued monetary easing, which should encourage 
additional investment. At the same time, downside risks remain as a result of ongoing uncertainties in consumer behavior, as 
reflected in subdued consumer confidence, and of potential restraint in business investment spending due to political 
uncertainty and continued high financing costs. A slow resurgence of inflation to a stable level could act as a further drag on 
growth. There are also external economic risks, such as geopolitical tensions, that could slow export growth.
For China, the IMF forecasts slight growth of 4.6% in 2025. Net exports should counteract the negative challenges posed by 
the ongoing real estate crisis and higher US tariffs. However, the situation on the real estate market could continue to 
deteriorate in the coming year, which would be likely to lead to price corrections and lower sales and investment. This would 
further weaken the already low consumer confidence, restrain consumption and slow domestic demand, which would also 
have a global impact. The government plans fiscal policy measures such as infrastructure investment and interest rate cuts to 
stabilize domestic demand.

Expected steel sector trend
For 2025, the World Steel Association forecasts slight growth in global steel demand by 1.2% to around 1,772 million tons. 
Slight growth of 1.6% is expected in the North American free trade area (USMCA) and of 4.8% in South and Central America. 
A slight 3.5% increase in demand for steel is forecast for the European Union together with the United Kingdom. In contrast, 
demand in China is expected to fall slightly by 1.0% in 2025 compared to the prior year.
5.2 
Expected trend in our core customer sectors
Construction industry
Oxford Economics estimates that the US construction industry will grow slightly by around 2% in 2025. As in 2024, the sector 
is expected to benefit from easing inflationary pressure – which will stimulate residential construction – falling interest rates 
and the resulting decline in mortgage costs, and from investment under the Inflation Reduction Act. High material costs are 
likely to remain a burden on the sector. The construction industry in the eurozone is expected to grow slightly by around 1%, 
while a slight decline (of 1%) is forecast for the construction industry in Switzerland. Positive factors include high population 
growth due to migration and falling interest rates, which should benefit the sector. However, the ongoing structural labour 
shortage remains a major challenge that is constraining growth. 
Machinery and mechanical engineering
Oxford Economics forecasts that the US machinery and mechanical engineering industry will show slight growth of 4% in 
2025. Falling interest rates have a positive effect and encourage investment, which should additionally stimulate demand. 
Furthermore, customer sectors of the mechanical and plant engineering industry are expected to see a recovery in the coming 
year, which should further increase demand. A slight increase of around 2% is expected for the sector in the eurozone. Here, 
too, falling interest rates are seen as the main driver, as they encourage investment. However, a rapid and broad-based 
recovery is not forecast in the short term, as there is expected to be a time lag between the change in interest rates and when 
it actually has an effect on the economy. 
Automotive industry
According to estimates by the German Association of the Automotive Industry (VDA), the US automotive industry will grow 
slightly by around 2% in 2025. The gradual easing of monetary policy and the associated interest rate cuts for car loans are 
expected to lend impetus to the sector in 2025. Oxford Economics forecasts slight growth of around 1% for the Mexican 
automotive industry in 2025. For the automotive industry in Europe, the VDA forecasts slight growth of 2% in 2025. Expected 
real wage increases should favor demand for automobiles, although consumer confidence remains subdued. The transition to 
electric vehicles continues to be a key driver for the industry. Slight growth of 1% is forecast for China.
